## Citation verification

Every `file:line` above is checked by grep. The script and its log travel with this report:

- `citations.tsv` — `file<TAB>line<TAB>grep-pattern<TAB>revision` per row.
- `verify-citations.sh` — resolves each row at HEAD (`grep -n`) or, for rows marked `27e11161`, at that commit (`git show <rev>:<file> | grep -n`), and prints `OK`/`MISS`.
- `citation-check.log` — the run, whose last line is the tally.

Rows citing `27e11161` are the unmerged fence work (node 7) and are verified at that commit; the entry-point rows for node 5 cite the dotfiles reference outside this repository and are verified with `grep -n` against that path.

**Tally: `line-ok=73  absent-ok=1  path-ok=1  MISS=0` — every citation resolves.** The pass corrected three rows (an off-by-one in `_dispatch_lane_reading`'s key read, and both `sandbox_*` helper lines in `_backends.py`); the corrected rows are what this report cites, and re-running the script now reports zero misses. Two of the checks are absences rather than presences: `worker_exit` occurs nowhere under `reckon/` or `tests/` at HEAD (so §7 introduces the key), and the dotfiles reference carries no `spawn` verb (so the verb is new work).

## Evidence inputs for the coordinator

1. **The rename is live and the plan text is stale** (node 2): `790d5c57` renamed `skills/reckon-ship` → `skills/reckon-build`; §4's write paths as written point at a directory that does not exist at HEAD. Dispatch §4 with the renamed paths, or accept a worker writing a plan-literal path it cannot create.
2. **Node 7's work is not on main**: the fence + harness home live at `27e11161` (commits `c72c460e`, `ed6f3f3c`, `f073da71`, `7bdadce0`, `27e11161`), reachable from no ref; the merge order and the CODEX_HOME repair are spelled in followup `f-awcwyh-002`.
3. **§10 is one protocol split across two nodes** (4 and 5): the FIFO line format, the spec's `argv`, and `spawned.json` must be frozen before either dispatches, or the pair will disagree about the acknowledgement file.
4. **Node 4's own plan section names dotfiles as a write path** (line 665) **while the lead's decision at line 718 makes reckon the destination.** Both cannot be true in one wave; the coordinator should choose.
5. **`launch_plan`'s fence default at HEAD is `False`** (`reckon/_backends.py:1872`), so today's workers are unfenced: §1 of `a-worker-cannot-write-your-home` landed as a capability with no caller. Node 7 is the flip.
6. **Node 1 is §2, not §3/§4** — if the coordinator intended "the page paints from the index", the wanted node is §4 (`docs/ui/state-loader.js` + `reckon/serve.py`), whose write paths the plan names at line 97–102; §3 is the index module `reckon/metadata_index.py`.

check-a-busy-member-launch-holds — delivered report

Cited from /home/ITER/mcintos/.config/reckon/crew/reports/reckon/s22-coord/check-a-busy-member-launch-holds/report.md.

# Report — a dispatch onto a member whose run still holds its session; observe on a zero-length stream

- Revision measured: `657e199ba75b7bcdfc67cadfd37592cecc68a19c`
  (the worktree's `git rev-parse HEAD`; the gate log header names it)
- Worktree: `/home/ITER/mcintos/Code/.reckon-worktrees/reckon-c8f839407e49/s22-coord/check-a-busy-member-launch-holds`
- Authority: `docs/plans/a-dispatch-returns-without-its-worker.html` section 11 (its two clauses:
  a dispatch onto a member whose previous run still holds its session must hold or
  divert rather than dying at launch; and observe must read `stderr.log` on a
  zero-length stream so a launch failure classifies `launch-failed` with its
  reason rather than as an orphan).
- This node changed no source. Both verdicts below are about the code as it
  stands at the named revision.

## Half 1 — dispatch onto a member whose previous run still holds its session

**Verdict: unmet as written.** A hold exists only for a *named* member whose
pointer is *live and non-terminal*, it refuses rather than waits or diverts, and
a terminal stored phase releases a live worker without a process check — so the
exact case in the clause (a run that has recorded its end while its worker
process still holds the session) is launched onto.

Code path at `657e199ba`:

1. The only member guard on the dispatch path is `reckon/crew/dispatch.py:6757-6765`:
   it builds `live_pointers` (excluding its own `run_id`) and, only when
   `roster_member is not None`, calls
   `refuse_member_in_flight(effective_member, pointer)` for a pointer whose
   `member` matches. An unnamed dispatch binds a disposable identity
   (`dispatch.py:6679`, `effective_member = member or _disposable_member_id(run_id)`),
   which can never match a prior pointer, so the guard is a no-op for it.
2. The verdict is taken entirely in `member_in_flight_verdict`
   (`reckon/crew/node.py:342-377`), reached through `refuse_member_in_flight`
   (`node.py:380-393`). Its arm order is decisive: a stored phase in
   `_TERMINAL_RUN_PHASES = {"complete", "failed", "stopped", "interrupted"}`
   (`node.py:32-34`) returns `blocks=False` **before any process check**
   (`node.py:355-361`). Only a non-terminal pointer is judged by liveness —
   alive blocks, proven gone releases, unknown blocks.
3. Nothing waits and nothing diverts. The guard raises `MemberInFlight`
   (`node.py:396-417`, the "already holds in-flight run" refusal); there is no
   wait-for-release and no fresh-session substitution anywhere. Session reuse is
   decided in `_task_session_resolution` (`dispatch.py:10922-10983`) from
   `_prior_same_task_run` (`dispatch.py:10887-10919`), whose only disqualifiers
   are a harness mismatch and `_session_too_large_to_continue`
   (`dispatch.py:10829-10884`) — liveness of the prior run is never consulted,
   and there is no fallback that starts a fresh session when the held one cannot
   be used. The one spawn retry that exists,
   `_spawn_worker_retrying_a_vanished_bind` (used at `dispatch.py:9572-9628` and
   in `_spawn_detached_worker`), retries only a vanished bind source; a launch
   that dies because the session is held is retried nowhere.

Consequence: the clause's scenario — dispatch twenty seconds after the prior run
finished, with the worker process or the backend's session writer still holding
the session — passes the guard, because the terminal arm releases without
asking, and launches onto the held session. `launch-failed` is not in
`_TERMINAL_RUN_PHASES`, so even that phase does not route into the by-liveness
arm.

Tests asserting the current behaviour at this revision:

- `tests/test_member_guard_counts_live_processes.py:134`,
  `test_a_terminal_phase_releases_a_live_worker_without_asking` — asserts
  `blocks is False` and `liveness == "terminal"` **with `pid=os.getpid()`**,
  i.e. a live worker. This is the gap, asserted as intended.
- `tests/test_dispatch_guard_consults_liveness.py:126` (live → refused, match
  "alive"), `:135` (gone → admitted), `:145` (unknown → refused), `:152`
  (dispatch wires the refusal), plus
  `tests/test_member_guard_counts_live_processes.py:89/95/110/116/146/159/164`.
- For the wait/divert half there is no test, because there is no code.

What a node would change: make the session-reuse decision consult the prior
run's *held session* rather than its stored phase — evaluate liveness
(three-valued, as the guard already does) even when the phase reads terminal —
and on a detected hold either wait for release or launch a fresh session and
report the substitution (the clause admits both). `_prior_same_task_run` is the
seam; it currently ignores liveness entirely.

## Half 2 — observe on a zero-length stream

**Verdict: unmet.** `observe` never reads `stderr.log`, and the zero-length
fold additionally *destroys* a launch-failed verdict that was already recorded:
`launch-failed` is not terminal, so observe overwrites it with `orphaned`. On
the reaper path that converts a launch failure with its stderr reason into
`interrupted` with no reason — the "indistinguishable from an orphan" reading,
produced by observe itself.

Code path at `657e199ba`:

1. `observe()` — `reckon/crew/dispatch.py:10556-10670`, CLI branch
   `10584-10631`. The zero-length-stream + dead-process arm is `10616-10631`:
   it sets `record["phase"] = "orphaned"` and the detail
   `"process exited without a terminal event in its log; check {stderr_path}"`.
   `stderr_path` is only *named* in that string; the file is never opened.
2. The observe fold's readers are the event stream and the codex login record
   (`reckon/_backends.py:3950-3995`, `observe_log`). On the crew side only
   `_launch_failure_record` (`dispatch.py:7624-7662`, reads stderr at `7642-7647`
   into `stderr_tail`) and `_died_over_a_vanished_bind_source`
   (`dispatch.py:8709-8741`) read `stderr.log` at all.
3. Launch-failure recording: `_record_launch_failure`
   (`dispatch.py:7665-7737`) appends the `launch_failures` entry and sets phase
   `launch-failed`. It runs only on the reaper path (callers at `dispatch.py:7765`
   and `8827`), not on the supervised path (`9572-9628`), where the end is the
   supervisor's exit record (`_supervisor_exit_record`, `9705-9735` — exit
   code/signal/records seen/`ended_during`, no stderr text) and the phase comes
   from `_publish_stored_phase` (`9850-9885`) → `_delivered_phase`
   (`9817-9847`).
4. Classification: `reckon/crew/recovery.py:8436-8477`. The launch-failed arm
   takes its reason from `launch_failures[-1]["stderr_tail"]` (`8445-8457`)
   when present, else the generic "no model was reached" text (`8468-8472`).
   The exit-record rescue is `recovery.py:6707-6750`
   (`_exit_record_is_launch_failure`, `6743`). `observe`'s fold is protected
   only against clobbering the terminal four (`_terminal_phase_survives`,
   `dispatch.py:10538-10553`), so `launch-failed` is overwritten.

Measured evidence (this node's own, in `probes/`):

- `probes/observe_zero_length_probe.py` builds three pointer shapes in a
  temporary `RECKON_HOME`, each twice — `stderr.log` holding
  `thread-store conflict: thread abc already has an active writer` versus the
  recorded `stderr_path` absent — and drives `observe` then
  `classify_pointer`:

  | case | observed phase | classification | reason in output |
  |---|---|---|---|
  | plain (working, nothing recorded) | `orphaned` | `interrupted` ("no terminal event … had already recorded") | no |
  | reaper (`launch-failed` + `launch_failures` entry) | `orphaned` | `interrupted` | no |
  | supervised (`launch-failed` + exit record `ended_during=launch`) | `orphaned` | `launch-failed`, generic "no model was reached" | no |

  Results are identical with and without the stderr file in all three cases —
  the fold never read it. Log: `probes/observe_zero_length_probe.log`.
- `probes/classify_without_observe.log` is the control: the same reaper pointer
  classified **directly** (`classify_pointer`, no observe) reads
  `launch-failed` with the reason attached —
  "(thread-store conflict: thread abc already has an active writer); 1 launch
  failure recorded". So the `interrupted` verdict is produced by the observe
  fold, not by the pointer's content: the reason exists on the pointer, in
  `launch_failures[].stderr_tail`, and the fold neither reads it nor preserves
  the phase that makes it reachable.

Tests asserting the current behaviour at this revision:

- `tests/test_crew_launch_failure.py:286`,
  `test_an_empty_stream_is_recorded_once_and_stops_the_lift` — asserts
  `"cannot run the backend" in failure["stderr_tail"]`: the reason is captured
  at *record* time on the reaper path (this test drives the recorder, not the
  observe flow). `:117` is the non-empty-stream control; `:312` the
  double-record guard; `:346` the lift refusal.
- `tests/test_recovery_reads_the_exit_record.py:183` (the launch-failure
  parameter case, `stream_records_seen=0`) — `launch-failed` from the exit
  record; `:349`
  `test_a_launch_failed_pointer_with_nothing_recorded_still_classifies` builds
  with `launch="in-harness"` (line 365), which skips the CLI observe arm
  entirely — no test exercises the clobber path.
- `tests/test_stored_phase_advances.py:222` / `:256` — the stored phase stays
  `launch-failed` for a spawn failure and an abandoned launch (record time,
  not observe).

What a node would change: before the zero-length arm writes `orphaned`, consult
the run's own launch account — the `launch_failures` entry and/or `stderr.log` —
as the clause says, keep a stored `launch-failed` phase through the fold, and
attach the stderr tail as the classification's reason.

## Gate

Command (worktree at `657e199ba`; header in the log names revision, tree, cwd,
module and the command):

```
PYTHONPATH=<worktree> RECKON_HOME=<scratch>/reckon-gate-home \
  /home/ITER/mcintos/Code/reckon/.venv/bin/python -m pytest -p no:cacheprovider -q \
  tests/test_crew_launch_failure.py tests/test_stored_phase_advances.py \
  tests/test_member_guard_counts_live_processes.py tests/test_dispatch_guard_consults_liveness.py \
  tests/test_recovery_reads_the_exit_record.py tests/test_resume_refuses_a_live_worker.py
```

Result: **42 passed in 18.52s, EXIT=0**. Log:
`gates/guard_gate.log`.

## Caveats

- The probe synthesises pointer shapes; it does not reproduce a live held-session
  collision end-to-end (that needs a second worker holding a real thread store).
  The code-path reading supplies the rest of half 1.
- `tests/test_dispatch_guard_consults_liveness.py:152` asserts the wiring by
  counting the string `refuse_member_in_flight` in `dispatch.py`'s source text
  rather than by behaviour — relevant to any node that later moves that call.
- `reckon crew check-manifest` on this run reports one finding, "status is
  complete but no commit is recorded": a false positive for an `investigate`
  run with a clean worktree and all `changed_paths` outside the repository —
  `reckon/crew/reports.py:1044-1058` exempts only the `review` role, while
  `reckon/crew/promotion.py:498-501` (and `2576+`) treats `investigate` as
  commitless for exactly this shape. Recorded under `follow_ons`; not fixed
  here (outside this node's write fence), and no commit is cited because this
  run made none.

§12 — The supervisor-finished-launch test holds under parallel load

reckon-build · 2026-10-04T13:25:40.874Z

review of r-20261004T121207607289 stored as a parsed 5-dimension record (goal_fidelity 19, evidence 16, scope_discipline 20, durability 18, fit 19; total 92) at reviewed_base_sha e49e4731a and reviewed_head_sha 89e4c221c; added_failure_count 0, added_failure_ids []; independently reproduced the primary test (base 7 pass/1 fail, head 8 pass/0 fail) and found the manifest's sibling 'head 20/20' claim does not reproduce at higher contention (head 4 pass/6 fail at line 1199).

reckon-build · 2026-10-04T13:04:27.557Z

tests/test_dispatch_returns_without_worker.py: both cases that wait on a supervisor now take its pid from the record dispatch returned (the live pointer's pid has two writers, and the supervisor's locked phase publish can carry back the pid-less record it read), and the completion exit record is waited for with a bound of max(10 s, 1.5 x the launch dispatch itself measured) instead of a fixed 10 s window. Contended measure for test_dispatch_returns_when_its_supervisor_finished_the_launch: 20 runs, 8 concurrent, beside 8 CPU-bound spinners -- base 13 pass / 7 fail, head 20 pass / 0 fail; sibling test_a_discarded_run_is_not_recreated, same recipe: base 14 pass / 6 fail, head 20 pass / 0 fail.

s22-coord · 2026-10-04T16:30:00Z

The pid-read race landed at merge 5c86f0b93, recorded as a failed gate classified pre-existing. Both supervisor tests take the pid from the record dispatch returned, because the live pointer's pid has two writers. They bound the exit-record wait by max(10 s, 1.5 x the measured launch). Under 8-way contention beside 8 CPU spinners, the target test went from 13 of 20 to 20 of 20. At load near 50 the coordinator's targeted gate still failed once in six, in the sibling test_a_discarded_run_is_not_recreated at its discard assertion, as the review reproduced. That is a product-side lost update which fails at base too, now §13. A third test, test_dispatch_returns_once_its_supervisor_runs, fails 3 of 3 at load near 50 on both base and head against its fixed 8 s exit bound; that is §14.

It checked that the pointer existed and then wrote it, and the launch-claim release unlinked the pointer without taking the per-run lock, so a release that landed between the check and the write lost. _release_launch_claim now takes _pointer_lock, so a publish already in progress finishes first and its existence check then refuses. crew discard already held that lock. test_a_phase_publish_does_not_restore_a_released_claim forces the interleaving with events and failed 20 of 20 at base, passing 20 of 20 at head beside eight CPU spinners. §14 — The dispatch-exit bound reads the machine it runs on

reckon-build · 2026-10-04T17:32:04.833Z

The dispatch return bound in tests/test_dispatch_returns_without_worker.py derives from a reference measured on the dispatch's own host at the moment of the dispatch (slowest of 3 fresh-interpreter imports of reckon.crew.dispatch, and the driver's startup_seconds, whichever is slower; bound = SUPERVISOR_SURVIVAL_SECONDS + 8.0 * reference), attested by 20 runs beside 8 CPU-bound spinners passing 20 of 20 at head, a declared mutation joining the supervisor failing the case, and a zero failure delta for the dispatch-subject importing population against base 0513a121e; fragment docs/evidence/fragments/a-dispatch-returns-without-its-worker/the-dispatch-exit-bound-reads-its-machine.html.

the-dispatch-followups-are-triaged — delivered report

Cited from /home/ITER/mcintos/.config/reckon/crew/reports/reckon/s21-1c/the-dispatch-followups-are-triaged/report.md.

# Triage of the open followups of `a-dispatch-returns-without-its-worker`

Base: `4406a8d02c9b5c8bc88a50790281748aad649106` (worktree
`/home/ITER/mcintos/Code/.reckon-worktrees/reckon-c8f839407e49/s21-1c/the-dispatch-followups-are-triaged`,
clean; no tracked file under `/home/ITER/mcintos/Code` was changed).
Plan: `docs/plans/a-dispatch-returns-without-its-worker.html`, followups at
lines 1002-1053. Each verdict is made against the committed blob at the base
unless a test run is named.

Evidence gathered by reading the named files, by one live probe of the
classifier for the pre-spawn case (`classification: running`,
`fleet_verdict.state: working`, `effective_phase: launching` with a live
supervisor pid, no stream, no `worker.json`), and by one command reproduction
of the runpy warning at base. Every cited commit resolves with
`git cat-file -e` in `/home/ITER/mcintos/Code/reckon`.

## Verdict table

| followup | verdict |
|---|---|
| f-adrwiw-handoff-01 | met |
| f-adrwiw-launch-flicker | met |
| f-adrwiw-runpy-warning | **unmet** |
| f-adrwiw-in-progress-unreadable | met |
| f-adrwiw-stop-window | **unmet** |
| f-adrwiw-remaining-after-audit | **unmet** (its §4 item; §6, §8, §10 halves are landed or reassigned) |
| f-adrwiw-retired-cap-reads-retired-everywhere | **unmet** |
| f-adrwiw-validation-assertion-reads-the-key | **unmet** |

Three of eight hold at base; five are open, four of them small (under two
worker-hours each).

---

## f-adrwiw-handoff-01 — met

Done-when, quoted from the plan (line 1004): *"**Done when** a sweep-dispatched
review's pointer carries the pid of the process that runs it, and a manual
dispatch of a review the reflex already launched is refused with the reflex's
run id, not a worktree error."*

Clause 1 — the pointer carries the pid of the process that runs the attempt.
The pointer names the supervisor and `worker.json` names the worker, by
design: `reckon/crew/recovery.py:4330-4336` (comment: "A supervised launch
writes the supervisor's pid on the pointer and the worker's pid here").
`reckon/crew/dispatch.py:7344-7364` (`_supervisor_argv`) builds the supervisor
vector, and `reckon/crew/dispatch.py:7696-7730` (`_supervisor_spawn_worker`)
forks the worker inside the supervisor's group, whose pid is then written to
the worker record. A review dispatch reaches that supervised path rather than
the in-process one: `reckon/crew/dispatch.py:7521-7540`
(`supervised_launch`) states that "a resumption and a review dispatch reach the
supervisor through here rather than through an in-process `_spawn` call", and
the sweep passes its launcher through `reckon/crew/recovery.py:7977`
(`dispatch_review_for_run(..., launcher=launcher)`) while
`reckon/cli.py:5515` calls `recover` with no launcher, so the default
(supervised) branch runs. The assertion:
`tests/test_every_attempt_is_supervised.py:173` —
`assert runs.read_pointer(run_id)["pid"] == supervisor_pid`, with
`:155` asserting the worker record names a different pid.

Caveat, stated so the row is not read as stronger than it is: no test asserts
the pid on a *reflex-dispatched review* specifically; the property is asserted
for a supervised attempt in general, and the reflex path is shown to take that
route by the code above.

Clause 2 — a hand dispatch racing the reflex is refused naming the claimant.
`tests/test_scope_refusal_names_its_claimant.py:228`
(`test_a_refusal_names_the_claimant_apart_from_the_refused_call`) and `:252`
name the claimant apart from the refused call; the module docstring is the
incident itself ("a coordinator whose hand dispatch was refused by a run its
own review reflex had launched read the refusal as its own dispatch conflicting
with itself"), landed `119253f50` (2026-09-28).
`tests/test_one_of_two_racing_reviews_survives.py:254` asserts
`refusal.value.run_id == peer_run_id` and that the refused dispatch left no
pointer — a named run id, not a worktree error. `a8010923b` (2026-10-01)
closed the remaining hole by keying "launched" on the phase rather than on a
pointer naming a worktree or pid, so a running owner is never outranked.

## f-adrwiw-launch-flicker — met

Done-when, quoted from the plan (line 1009): *"Done when a run whose recorded
supervisor pid is alive and which has no `worker.json` yet classifies as
launching, never abandoned, in both the follower and the live view. A test
holds the supervisor in a stubbed snapshot and asserts no abandoned transition
is emitted."*

- The pre-spawn phases and the rule that the launcher's label stands until a
  run's own evidence advances it: `reckon/crew/recovery.py:4331`
  (`_PRE_SPAWN_PHASES`) and `reckon/crew/recovery.py:4334-4395`
  (`_observed_phase`).
- Abandoned requires proof of death — the reducer reaches it only under
  `alive is False` (`reckon/crew/recovery.py:6952-6974`), and the classifier's
  terminal arms read a live process as running (`recovery.py:6091-6100`,
  `:6140-6150`).
- Test: `tests/test_a_live_run_never_reads_dead.py:346`
  (`test_a_pre_spawn_launch_is_not_abandoned`) holds a live child as the
  supervisor pid, phase `starting`, `write_stream=False` (no stream and no
  worker record — the pre-spawn window), and asserts
  `classification == "running"`, `!= "abandoned"`. Landed 2026-09-25
  (`8e22b2b86`, `11ad0a896`, `56002462c`); the module docstring names "a launch
  flicker read as abandoned" as one of the misreads it closes.
- No abandoned transition: `tests/test_a_live_run_never_reads_dead.py:462`
  asserts the exact state sequence
  `("dispatched", "working", "completed_unpromoted")` as the launch advances,
  so an abandoned state anywhere in it would fail the equality.
- Both surfaces agree: `tests/test_producer_shares_the_classifier_verdict.py:144`
  (`test_every_liveness_stub_has_producer_classifier_parity`) checks every stub
  in the liveness module for producer/classifier parity, and `:208` pins that
  the snapshot and the emitted transition do not re-derive the verdict.
- Independent probe at base (scratch script under `$TMPDIR`, no repo write):
  pointer phase `launching`, pid = a live process, no stream / no `worker.json`
  / no manifest → `classification: running`, `effective_phase: launching`,
  `fleet_verdict.state: working` (`working` ∈ `FLEET_WORKING_STATES`,
  `reckon/crew/recovery.py:7181`).

Note on wording: the display state for a pre-spawn run is `dispatched`
(`tests/test_a_finished_run_never_reads_dispatched.py:207-215`), not the word
"launching"; the substance the done-when requires — never abandoned while the
recorded process is alive — holds on both surfaces.

## f-adrwiw-runpy-warning — unmet

Done-when, quoted from the plan (line 1014): *"Done when the supervisor
launches through an entry point whose module is not already imported, and a
launched supervisor's stderr log is empty."*

Reproduced at base:

```
$ PYTHONPATH=<worktree> /home/ITER/mcintos/Code/reckon/.venv/bin/python -m reckon.crew.dispatch
<frozen runpy>:128: RuntimeWarning: 'reckon.crew.dispatch' found in sys.modules
after import of package 'reckon.crew', but prior to execution of
'reckon.crew.dispatch'; this may result in unpredictable behaviour
```

The launch vector is unchanged: `reckon/crew/dispatch.py:7344-7364`
(`_supervisor_argv`) returns
`[sys.executable, "-m", "reckon.crew.dispatch", SUPERVISOR_ENTRY, "--spec", …]`
with `SUPERVISOR_ENTRY = "__supervise__"` (`dispatch.py:7031`). The
`__supervise__` sub-command does not help: runpy emits the warning from the
`-m` form before the module body runs. There is no console-script supervisor
entry (`pyproject.toml:47-48` declares only `reckon = "reckon.cli:main"`), and
the same vector is written into the spec for the fleet batch step
(`dispatch.py:7336-7337`), so the fix has to change both.

Smallest remaining change: add a supervisor entry module that nothing imports
(e.g. `reckon/crew/supervisor_main.py` whose body is
`raise SystemExit(_supervisor_command(sys.argv[1:]))`), point
`_supervisor_argv` at it, and add a case asserting a launched supervisor's
stderr log is empty. Estimate: 1-2 worker-hours (new module, one vector, one
test, plus the fleet-spec path which reads the same function).

## f-adrwiw-in-progress-unreadable — met

The followup states no "Done when" sentence; the end state it asks for is the
operative sentence (plan line 1019): *"The follower renders that run as
`unreadable` with a <q>repair: the manifest at …</q> remedy, which looks like a
delivery defect when it is a healthy checkpoint."* The reading to remove is:
`in-progress` must not render as `unreadable`.

- `reckon/crew/reports.py:88-89` — `NON_TERMINAL_MANIFEST_STATUSES` carries
  `in-progress`, `in_progress`, `running`, `pending`.
- `reckon/crew/recovery.py:5982-5993` — the non-terminal arm reads a
  worker-reported working status as evidence of life and classifies the run
  `running`, "never abandoned, whatever state the process is in"; the
  unreadable arms (`:5812`, `:5968-5981`) are reached only for a manifest that
  cannot be read or a broken wait declaration, not for a recognised status.
- Test: `tests/test_unreadable_names_its_word.py:231`
  (`test_a_recognised_non_terminal_manifest_beside_a_live_worker_is_silent`)
  drives the watch surface (`runs._project_watch_claim` + `crew.list_live`)
  and asserts the emitted events are exactly
  `[("baseline", None, "working")]` — no unreadable transition, no repair
  remedy — with `classification == "running"` and `in-progress` carried on the
  row. Landed `aaefa3773` (2026-09-28).
- All four spellings are pinned at `tests/test_recovery_state_typing.py:319-347`;
  the underlying rule (non-terminal status ⇒ running) landed earlier in
  `e662009cb` (2026-09-14).

## f-adrwiw-stop-window — unmet

The followup states no single "Done when" sentence; it carries three residual
findings (plan line 1030) whose end state is: *(1)* the stop flag is re-checked
under the same signal mask immediately before the fork, or SIGTERM is blocked
across the check-and-fork; *(2)* a test delivers a group stop to a live
supervisor after its worker spawns and asserts the recorded exit; *(3)* the
changelog phrase is restated as the invariant. All three are open at base.

1. Single read, no re-check. `reckon/crew/dispatch.py:7883` installs the
   handler and returns the flag; `dispatch.py:7886` is the only read
   (`if stop_requested.is_set():`), and the spawn follows at `:7907`.
   `_supervisor_spawn_worker` (`dispatch.py:7696-7730`) contains no second
   check and no `signal.pthread_sigmask` block, so a SIGTERM delivered after
   the check and before `Popen` still spawns a worker.
2. No post-spawn group-stop case.
   `tests/test_stop_before_spawn_spawns_nothing.py:293` is the file's only
   test and covers the pre-spawn snapshot only.
   `tests/test_every_attempt_is_supervised.py:239` kills the *worker* by pid
   and reads the exit, then stops the group — it does not model a group stop
   delivered while the worker is alive, which is the case the finding asks
   for (a supervisor that exits instead of recording the stop's exit would
   still pass).
3. The phrase is still present: `tests/test_stop_before_spawn_spawns_nothing.py:7`
   reads "It used to be ignored: the supervisor survived the signal, finished
   its snapshot, and spawned the worker anyway". The finding asked for the
   invariant ("a stop delivered inside that window means no worker is ever
   spawned") in place of the changelog clause. Introduced by `d47c6a700`
   (2026-09-25), carried by `7827b4ebc`.

Smallest remaining change: in `_run_supervisor`, block SIGTERM/SIGHUP around
the check-and-fork (or re-check the flag immediately before `Popen` under the
same mask); add a post-spawn group-stop case with a stub worker in
`tests/test_stop_before_spawn_spawns_nothing.py` asserting the exit record
(`signal_name` composition already exists at `dispatch.py:5951-5961`); restate
the test module docstring as the invariant. Each change with a negative
control. Estimate: 2-3 worker-hours.

reckon-ship · 2026-09-23T07:10:00Z

§3 as written would lose every worker's exit record. It is held until it is revised. The S19 session measured this on 2026-09-23 (worker-death-is-recorded §5, commit 2e5e8f53). launch_failures holds 0 exit records across every live pointer, and the ledger carries no signal_name, after a night of at least eight worker deaths. The reason: crew dispatch exits after launching, its reaper thread dies with it, and the worker is reparented to init (a live review worker read PPID 1), so init collects the status and reckon never sees it. §3 currently makes that path the design: a worker whose parent has exited is reparented and collected by the system. The serve logged 20,507 HTTP 200 responses and no non-200 over the same period, so the mid-thinking deaths are client-side, and this missing record is what would name them.

The revision proposed to S19 keeps one mechanism with one owner. Dispatch returns, and a detached per-run supervisor stays. The supervisor runs in its own session, spawns the worker, waits on it, records the exit status and signal into the pointer, and then exits. Dispatch returns once the supervisor reports the launch. The done-when would gain S19's measure: a real dispatch, the dispatching process gone, the worker SIGKILLed, and the exit status still recorded. Which session builds the supervisor is being agreed with S19. §3 is not dispatched until that is settled and the section text is revised.

reckon-ship · 2026-09-23T09:55:00Z

The hold is found, on live dispatches, where the probe's stub path could not reach it. The §2 probe (r-20260923T044807192716-dispatch-lifetime-probe) established that the worker survives SIGTERM, SIGKILL and SIGHUP to its dispatch process, and that the reaper thread is a daemon. It found no reckon frame holding its stub dispatch open. Its repository was temporary, held one worktree and had no remote. On 2026-09-23 at 09:50 UTC, py-spy dump --nonblocking was run against two live dispatches whose workers had already launched: nova, 24 minutes old, and imas-efit, 10 minutes old. Both main threads were in the same frame: dispatch at reckon/crew/dispatch.py:4002, record["repository_tree_snapshot"] = _repository_tree_snapshot(repo_root), then _repository_tree_snapshot at reckon/crew/routing.py:403, then _tree_state at routing.py:349, waiting on git status --porcelain=v1 -z --untracked-files=all --no-renames. The snapshot runs that command once in every registered worktree of the repository, one after another. Registered worktrees at the time: reckon 244, nova 308, imas-efit 191, imas-ambix 161. One reckon worktree's status took 3.3 s, so the hold is about 13 minutes on reckon. That matches this session's own dispatch, which launched its worker at 09:32:41 UTC and returned at 09:48:38 UTC. The hold grows with every worktree left behind, which is why §1 measured six minutes on 2026-09-22 and it is sixteen now.

Two consequences for §3. First, the snapshot is a boundary baseline, and its comment requires it to be taken after dispatch's own writes. It must also predate any write the worker makes in another tree, so it cannot simply move after dispatch returns without a race. Second, the exception handler around it at dispatch.py:4005 onward signals the worker's process group, so a snapshot that raises after launch kills a launched worker and returns a refusal. That is a candidate cause of §6's refusal beside a live pointer.

reckon-ship · 2026-09-23T13:20:00Z

reckon-ship · 2026-09-23T14:25:00Z

Retiring the cap removes the only check dispatch makes against load, and that gap needs an owner. Three instances were measured on 2026-09-23. imas-ambix-61 at 13:17 UTC: refused at 65 of 64 with 23 live of 69 pointers. imas-ambix-b6 at 16:14 CEST: refused at 64 of 64 with 31 live of 74. This session, dispatching watch-lifetime-tests-read-the-canonical-state with a per-call --set backends.clive.max_concurrent_runs=1000 override at 14:19 UTC, sent the node into a lane reading running 29, headroom 0, concurrent_requests 23, with its router gate at 30 of 30 in flight. In b6's case the dead-pointer count refused for the wrong reason at a moment when the lane really was full. So once the cap is gone, nothing in crew dispatch consults the lane's own headroom, although dispatch already captures a lane reading and carries it on the run record (widened per field at 1a64dede). That advisory is advise-the-lane-never-force-it §2, The advisory arrives where the choice is made. It advises and never refuses, which fits the contract that no numeric worker cap exists. §8 does not wait on it: the router gate now answers overload with an explicit 529 and Retry-After, so an over-full lane shows as backpressure rather than silent degradation. Until §2 lands, this session's coordinator reads lane.json headroom before each dispatch and holds new work at zero.

reckon-ship · 2026-09-23T14:45:00Z

§5 landed, merged at 4248cbaf. The reproduction found the defect real. Of six refusal paths, two are reached after the pointer write, and both left a live pointer, its run directory and its worktree at base. The unwind removed the worktree first, and the remover refused because the run's own pointer still claimed it, so the rest of the unwind never ran. That is the mechanism of imas-ambix-61's refusing to remove worktree … claimed by live runs <its own run>. The pointer and run directory now go first. With the base dispatch.py restored, exactly those two cases fail and the four pre-pointer cases still pass. One observation is carried, not repaired: crew discard leaves a launch-failed run's worktree on disk, so a re-dispatch of the same node id is correctly refused; the tree is the discarding command's to remove. §6, a post-launch failure that kills the worker, is not addressed by this.

unknown · 2026-09-24

The landed supervisor does not survive a per-session SLURM step. This was measured by imas-ambix-af on 2026-09-24, ahead of moving the session fleet off the login node into the SLURM fleet allocation. Each session will run as its own step, and a setsid-detached child died with its step. §3's supervisor detaches with start_new_session=True, and so does the worker beneath it. On a login node that keeps them alive past dispatch's exit. Inside a session's step, both are reaped when the session ends, so a coordinator ending its turn would take every worker it dispatched with it. The worker's launch therefore has to leave the session's step: through a fleet supervisor running in the batch step, or through another path that does not keep the srun client in the session's step. This is a new section, not a revision of §3. §3's contract, dispatch returning once a supervisor is running, still holds; what changes is where that supervisor lives. The write-up is in imas-ambix's slurm-hosted-agent-fleet. Nothing dispatches on it until the fleet move lands and the lead's hold is lifted.

Simon McIntosh (via imas-ambix-af) · undated

Lead decisions for §10, 2026-09-24. (1) The spawn verb is authorised. (2) The lead questions why the fleet supervisor lives in dotfiles: it should be part of reckon or ambix. The supervisor is placement and launch machinery, and placement, spawn and the reservation record are reckon's, so the proposal is that §10 moves it into reckon (for example reckon fleet supervise, run as the batch step of the allocation reckon's ensure holds). ~/.local/state/fleet/record.json would be folded into reckon's placement record, leaving dotfiles with only the thin cx connection client, fleet-attach and fleet-shell. The dotfiles bin/fleet-supervisor (commits 3cfd155 onward) is the working reference: its FIFO protocol, its reload verb, its stripping of inherited ZELLIJ and CLAUDE variables, and its runtime directory under /tmp/<uid>-fleet. Confirm the destination with the lead before moving it.

Simon McIntosh (via imas-ambix-af) · undated

Lead decision on where the fleet pieces live, 2026-09-24: they are persisted when reckon is synced into a project, not by dotfiles. Reckon owns the supervisor (the batch step of its held allocation, carrying the spawn verb) and installs the connection helpers, fleet-attach and fleet-claude, through its sync. dotfiles keeps cx only as the terminal entry point, and cx reads reckon's placement record for the job id. The dotfiles copies from 3cfd155 onward are the reference implementation to move, not the destination.
